========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, hi. Thanks for seeing me. Well, what made me come in is that, for the past year or so, I just keep getting pulled off track. The smallest thing pulls me off what I’m doing before I can even stop it. Like a phone buzz, or somebody walking past my office. We just moved into this new commercial space downtown, actually. Great square footage, but it's got all these glass walls. My business partner thought it would look more open and collaborative, but honestly, I see every single shadow moving out there. 

Or, I don't know, even a random thought will just derail me. Like, I'll be looking at a spreadsheet and suddenly I'm thinking about my in-laws. Which, don't get me started, they're coming down next month and my father-in-law is already texting me about renting a boat. He always wants to get a pontoon, but he never offers to pay for the gas. It's ridiculous, the guy made a killing in real estate in the nineties but acts like a twenty-dollar gas can is going to bankrupt him. 

But... yeah, what was I saying? Right, I just get pulled away from my work by every little thing before I can catch myself. It's been happening a lot this past year.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, looking back, it was definitely around when I was a kid, too. I mean, my teachers were always moving my seat because I talked way too much, or I'd just get up and wander around without asking. 

I remember Mrs. Gable in... I think it was eighth grade? She actually moved my desk right next to her filing cabinet to keep an eye on me. Which was a terrible idea, honestly, because I just spent the whole time reading the labels on her folders. She had this one red folder that just said "Priority" and I always wanted to know what was in it. Probably just, like, detention slips or something. I actually ran into her a few years ago at a Home Depot, buying mulch. She looked exactly the same, just a little grayer. She was looking at the cedar chips, but I told her the dyed black mulch holds its color way better if your yard gets full sun. 

But yeah, I was always blurting things out before they even finished asking a question in class. School just wasn't really built for me, which is probably why I ended up leaving in tenth grade to just start working. Best decision I ever made, honestly. I actually ran into her a few years ago at a Home Depot, buying mulch. She looked exactly the same, just a little grayer. She was looking at the cedar chips, but I told her the dyed black mulch holds its color way better if your yard gets full sun. \n\nBut yeah, I was always blurting things out before they even finished asking a question in class. Well, yeah, actually. I mean, I've always been like this, like we talked about, but it's definitely been noticeably harder to manage for, I don't know, about a year and two months now? 

That's right around the time this whole massive conflict with my in-laws started taking over, like, our entire family life. It's just been non-stop drama. My wife's sister, she decided to buy this fixer-upper house right down the street from us, which sounds great in theory, right? Family close by. But she expected me to basically be her free general contractor because I run a business. I told her, look, I do junk removal and logistics, I don't hang drywall. But she got incredibly offended, and then my mother-in-law got involved, and suddenly we're the bad guys because we won't spend our weekends putting up shiplap. 

Shiplap is so overdone anyway, honestly. Every house you see on those home renovation shows has it now. My wife watches those channels constantly. We actually thought about flipping houses once, back before the market got totally crazy. We went to this seminar at a Holiday Inn out by the airport. You know, one of those things where they try to sell you a ten-thousand-dollar coaching package at the end? Total scam. But the coffee they had in the lobby was surprisingly good. Usually hotel coffee tastes like burnt dirt, but this was a really smooth dark roast. I actually asked the guy at the front desk what brand it was, but he had no idea. 

Anyway... wait, what was the question? Oh, right, the stress. Yeah, the in-law thing has just been a huge drain on everything for the last year or so, and it definitely made all this other stuff way harder to deal with.

Well, I mean, the in-law stuff definitely made it harder to deal with, but it's not like it just started then. Looking back, this was around when I was a kid, too. My teachers were always moving my seat because I talked way too much, or I'd just get up without asking, or I'd blurt things out before they even finished the question.

Actually, oak furniture is really making a massive comeback right now. We just bought this giant solid oak dining table for the house. We had to hire three guys just to carry it through the front door because it weighed like four hundred pounds. You wouldn't believe how many shades of white paint there are at the hardware store. My wife brought home like thirty little paper swatches—eggshell, alabaster, antique snow... it all looks exactly the same to me. I told her to just pick one so we could get it over with, but she spent three weeks deciding. 

Anyway, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, the in-laws and it getting worse. Yeah, so the stress with them just kind of amplified how I've always been since I was little.

It's ridiculous, the guy made a killing in real estate in the nineties but acts like a twenty-dollar gas can is going to bankrupt him. \n\nBut... yeah, what was I saying? I actually ran into her a few years ago at a Home Depot, buying mulch. She looked exactly the same, just a little grayer. She was looking at the cedar chips, but I told her the dyed black mulch holds its color way better if your yard gets full sun. \n\nBut yeah, I was always blurting things out before they even finished asking a question in class. I kind of lost my train of thought there.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend a lot of time worrying about different things most days?\n\nPatient: Worrying? No, not really. I don't really sit around and stew on things. If there's a problem, I just fix it or move on. Like, just last Tuesday, our main supplier for corrugated boxes tried to hit us with a fifteen percent price hike out of nowhere. I didn't even stress about it, I just immediately called up this guy I know in Omaha who runs a packaging plant. \n\nGreat guy, actually. I met him at a trade show in Vegas back in, like, 2018? Man, Vegas is wild. We stayed at the Bellagio, and their buffet used to be absolutely top tier, but that time we went, the crab legs were totally rubbery. You can always tell when they just leave them sitting under those heat lamps way too long. My wife loves crab, but she absolutely refuses to eat it unless we’re actually on the coast somewhere. Yeah, no, I'm not really a worrier. I just keep moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel physically restless or unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, absolutely. I'm always fidgeting or bouncing my leg. It just feels like there’s a motor running inside me, you know? The doctor said it would just pass on its own, but my wife was practically having a panic attack the whole time... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, sitting still. Yeah, I just can't do it. I always need to be doing something or moving around.\n\nDoctor: Before these last year of symptoms, was there any major stressful event or change in your life that seemed to trigger them?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. I mean, I've always been like this, like we talked about, but it's definitely been noticeably harder to manage for, I don't know, about a year and two months now? \n\nThat's right around the time this whole massive conflict with my in-laws started taking over, like, our entire family life. It's just been non-stop drama. My wife's sister, she decided to buy this fixer-upper house right down the street from us, which sounds great in theory, right? Family close by. But she expected me to basically be her free general contractor because I run a business. I told her, look, I do junk removal and logistics, I don't hang drywall. But she got incredibly offended, and then my mother-in-law got involved, and suddenly we're the bad guys because we won't spend our weekends putting up shiplap. \n\nShiplap is so overdone anyway, honestly. Every house you see on those home renovation shows has it now. My wife watches those channels constantly. We actually thought about flipping houses once, back before the market got totally crazy. We went to this seminar at a Holiday Inn out by the airport. You know, one of those things where they try to sell you a ten-thousand-dollar coaching package at the end? Total scam. But the coffee they had in the lobby was surprisingly good. Usually hotel coffee tastes like burnt dirt, but this was a really smooth dark roast. I actually asked the guy at the front desk what brand it was, but he had no idea. \n\nAnyway... wait, what was the question? Oh, right, the stress. Yeah, the in-law thing has just been a huge drain on everything for the last year or so, and it definitely made all this other stuff way harder to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have your attention and restlessness been mainly getting worse because of the in-law conflict?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, the in-law stuff definitely made it harder to deal with, but it's not like it just started then. Looking back, this was around when I was a kid, too. My teachers were always moving my seat because I talked way too much, or I'd just get up without asking, or I'd blurt things out before they even finished the question. \n\nI remember my fifth-grade teacher, Mrs. Henderson, she used to put my desk literally right next to hers. She had this huge wooden desk, solid oak, I think. Actually, oak furniture is really making a massive comeback right now. We just bought this giant solid oak dining table for the house. We had to hire three guys just to carry it through the front door because it weighed like four hundred pounds. One of the guys actually scraped the doorframe, which was incredibly annoying because I had just paid a guy to paint the trim this specific shade of white. You wouldn't believe how many shades of white paint there are at the hardware store. My wife brought home like thirty little paper swatches—eggshell, alabaster, antique snow... it all looks exactly the same to me. I told her to just pick one so we could get it over with, but she spent three weeks deciding. \n\nAnyway, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, the in-laws and it getting worse. Yeah, so the stress with them just kind of amplified how I've always been since I was little.\n\n[Last Question]\nDid these problems first begin after the conflict with your in-laws started?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 38 / chief_executive / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Additional Requirement 1\nmanifestation: \"Looking back, this was around when I was a kid too—teachers were always moving my seat because I talked too much, got up without asking, or blurted things out before they finished the question.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, no, like I was just saying a second ago, it definitely didn't start with the in-laws. Looking back, this was totally around when I was a kid, too. My teachers were always moving my seat around the classroom because I talked way too much, or I'd just get up without asking, or I'd be blurting things out before they even finished asking the question. 

I spent half my time sitting out in the hallway because of it. Our school hallways always smelled like that really strong industrial floor wax, you know the kind? Like fake lemon and heavy chemicals. We actually use a similar industrial degreaser at the warehouse for my company. I run a hauling and logistics business, which is great, but keeping the warehouse floors clean is a total nightmare with the trucks pulling in and out all day. The tires track in so much mud, especially when it rains. It rained for like four days straight last week, completely ruined my weekend plans. I was supposed to take my boat out to the lake. It’s just a small aluminum fishing boat, but I just put a new outboard motor on it, a Yamaha. Those Yamaha motors are incredibly reliable, way better than the Mercury I had before. The old one kept stalling out right in the middle of the lake. 

Wait, sorry. Did you just ask me the same question? But yeah, no, definitely not just the in-laws. Like I said, it's been happening since I was a little kid in school.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm pretty high-energy most of the time, but not like... unusually upbeat for days on end without sleeping. I definitely need my sleep. If I don't get at least six hours, I'm totally useless the next day. 

Although, honestly, getting decent sleep has been tough lately because we just got this new mattress. It's one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a box, which is wild to me. How do they compress a whole king-size mattress into a box that small? The delivery guy practically threw his back out carrying it up our front steps. We had to wait 48 hours for it to fully expand before we could even sleep on it, which meant we were stuck on the air mattress in the guest room for two nights. Air mattresses are the absolute worst. They always deflate right in the middle, so you wake up folded in half like a taco. I actually tried to patch a hole in an air mattress once with duct tape. Turns out, duct tape does not fix everything, despite what my dad used to say. He used duct tape on the bumper of his old Ford pickup for like three years. It was this hideous silver stripe across the back of a red truck. 

But yeah, anyway, no, I don't have periods where I'm just buzzing for days without sleep. Just my normal level of busy.

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, not really? I'm not a super anxious guy. I don't sit around worrying about a bunch of random things every day. I'm usually too busy doing stuff to just sit and worry. 

Though I guess I do worry a little bit about the supply chain issues with the business right now. It's been a total headache trying to get parts for our forklifts. We had one down for three weeks waiting on a hydraulic pump. Three weeks! And the guy at the parts distributor was zero help. He just kept saying it was stuck at the port. I actually drove down to the port once, years ago, just to see the shipping containers. It's insane how big those cranes are. They look like giant metal dinosaurs just picking up these massive boxes. My nephew is obsessed with dinosaurs right now. He's four, and he knows the names of all the weird ones, not just the T-Rex. He was telling me about one with a club on its tail... an Ankylosaurus, I think? I bought him this huge remote-control dinosaur for his birthday, but it was so loud it made his dog hide under the couch. The dog is a rescue, some kind of terrier mix, and he's terrified of loud noises. We had a terrier growing up, actually... 

Wait, what was the question? Worrying? Yeah, no, I don't really worry about a ton of stuff on a daily basis. Just normal business stuff, you know?

Not really, no. I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge most days. I mean, I'm always moving, always doing something, but it's not an anxious feeling. It's just... I just have a lot of energy, you know? 

Like, I can't just sit still. If I sit still too long, my legs start bouncing. It drives my wife crazy when we watch TV. We were watching this documentary the other night about deep-sea diving, which is fascinating, by the way. The amount of pressure down there is unbelievable. They have to use these special submersibles with super thick glass. I don't think I could ever do that, being trapped in a tiny metal ball underwater. I get a little claustrophobic sometimes. Like when I have to crawl under the deck to check the pipes. We had a pipe freeze last winter and it cracked, water everywhere. I had to army-crawl through the mud with a flashlight in my teeth. Reminded me of when I used to play paintball with my buddies back in my twenties. We used to go out into the woods every weekend. I was actually pretty good at it, very quick on my feet. 

But yeah, no, I'm not walking around feeling on edge or anything. Just, you know, always ready to go.

He just kept saying it was stuck at the port. I actually drove down to the port once, years ago, just to see the shipping containers. It's insane how big those cranes are. They look like giant metal dinosaurs just picking up these massive boxes. My nephew is obsessed with dinosaurs right now. He's four, and he knows the names of all the weird ones, not just the T-Rex. He was telling me about one with a club on its tail... an Ankylosaurus, I think? I bought him this huge remote-control dinosaur for his birthday, but it was so loud it made his dog hide under the couch. The dog is a rescue, some kind of terrier mix, and he's terrified of loud noises. We had a terrier growing up, actually... \n\nWait, what was the question? Worrying? Yeah, no, I don't really worry about a ton of stuff on a daily basis. Just normal business stuff, you know?\n\nDoctor: On most days, do you feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge most days. I mean, I'm always moving, always doing something, but it's not an anxious feeling. It's just... I just have a lot of energy, you know? \n\nLike, I can't just sit still. If I sit still too long, my legs start bouncing. It drives my wife crazy when we watch TV. We were watching this documentary the other night about deep-sea diving, which is fascinating, by the way. The amount of pressure down there is unbelievable. They have to use these special submersibles with super thick glass. I don't think I could ever do that, being trapped in a tiny metal ball underwater. I get a little claustrophobic sometimes. Like when I have to crawl under the deck to check the pipes. We had a pipe freeze last winter and it cracked, water everywhere. I had to army-crawl through the mud with a flashlight in my teeth. Reminded me of when I used to play paintball with my buddies back in my twenties. We used to go out into the woods every weekend. I was actually pretty good at it, very quick on my feet. \n\nBut yeah, no, I'm not walking around feeling on edge or anything. Just, you know, always ready to go.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you find that you worry a lot about many different things and have trouble controlling that worry?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 38 / chief_executive / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really. Like I said, I'm not a big worrier. I don't get stuck on things or feel like I can't control it. I mean, I have a lot on my plate, running the company and all, but I don't sit around chewing my fingernails over every little detail. If there's a problem, I just fix it and move on. 

Like last week, the landscaping guys we use at the main warehouse accidentally backed their riding mower into our chainlink fence. Smashed it pretty good. The owner of the landscaping company, Sal, he's a great guy though. He makes his own venison sausage. He actually brought me a whole cooler full of it last November. I've never really gotten into hunting myself, honestly. You have to sit perfectly still in a freezing cold tree stand for like, six hours straight just waiting for a deer to walk by. I would go absolutely out of my mind. I'd much rather be on a snowmobile or a dirt bike, you know? Something with a throttle. I actually rebuilt a 1998 Honda dirt bike in my garage last spring. Took it completely apart down to the frame, repainted it, the whole nine yards. Getting the carburetor tuned right was a nightmare, but man, it runs like a dream now. 

Wait, sorry, what did you ask me? About worrying? Yeah, no, I don't really have a problem with worrying too much. I just stay busy.

It's just been non-stop drama. My wife's sister, she decided to buy this fixer-upper house right down the street from us, which sounds great in theory, right? Family close by. Yeah, so the stress with them just kind of amplified how I've always been since I was little.\n\nDoctor: Did these problems first begin after the conflict with your in-laws started?\n\nPatient: No, no, like I was just saying a second ago, it definitely didn't start with the in-laws. Looking back, this was totally around when I was a kid, too. The tires track in so much mud, especially when it rains. It rained for like four days straight last week, completely ruined my weekend plans. I was supposed to take my boat out to the lake. It’s just a small aluminum fishing boat, but I just put a new outboard motor on it, a Yamaha. Those Yamaha motors are incredibly reliable, way better than the Mercury I had before. The old one kept stalling out right in the middle of the lake. \n\nWait, sorry. Did you just ask me the same question? But yeah, no, definitely not just the in-laws. Like I said, it's been happening since I was a little kid in school.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually energized or overly upbeat, with much less need for sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm pretty high-energy most of the time, but not like... unusually upbeat for days on end without sleeping. I definitely need my sleep. If I don't get at least six hours, I'm totally useless the next day. \n\nAlthough, honestly, getting decent sleep has been tough lately because we just got this new mattress. It's one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a box, which is wild to me. How do they compress a whole king-size mattress into a box that small? The delivery guy practically threw his back out carrying it up our front steps. We had to wait 48 hours for it to fully expand before we could even sleep on it, which meant we were stuck on the air mattress in the guest room for two nights. Air mattresses are the absolute worst. They always deflate right in the middle, so you wake up folded in half like a taco. I actually tried to patch a hole in an air mattress once with duct tape. Turns out, duct tape does not fix everything, despite what my dad used to say. He used duct tape on the bumper of his old Ford pickup for like three years. Yeah, no, I don't really worry about a ton of stuff on a daily basis. Just normal business stuff, you know?\n\nDoctor: On most days, do you feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. No, not really. I mean, nothing traumatic or anything like that. Things have just always been kind of... fast-paced for me, I guess. I've always been this way, even back in high school. Well, the part of high school I actually went to before I dropped out to work full-time.

We took an RV up to Maine a couple years ago just for the seafood, actually. Driving an RV is a lot harder than it looks, by the way, especially trying to park a thirty-footer at a Cracker Barrel... \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask me? Oh, right, worrying. Yeah, no, I'm not really a worrier. I just keep moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel physically restless or unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, absolutely. I'm always fidgeting or bouncing my leg. Like, when I'm supposed to sit still, especially during those long family dinners or... oh, I volunteer with the finance committee at our church, and those meetings are brutal. It just feels like there’s a motor running inside me, you know? I just have to move or find something to do with my hands. \n\nActually, at the last church meeting, I ended up completely taking apart my ballpoint pen and putting it back together like five times. It was one of those nice metal ones, too, a Pilot G2 Pro. Great pen, honestly, the ink flow is so smooth. I buy them in bulk for the office because the cheap plastic ones always dry out or the caps crack. My business partner, he always chews on the caps of his pens. Drives me insane. I told him he's going to swallow a piece of plastic one day and end up in the ER. \n\nSpeaking of the ER, my youngest son, he's eight, he actually had to go last month because he swallowed a quarter. A whole quarter! Just popped it in his mouth on a dare from his cousin. Kids are unbelievable. The doctor said it would just pass on its own, but my wife was practically having a panic attack the whole time... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, sitting still. Yeah, I just can't do it. I always need to be doing something or moving around.\n\nDoctor: Before these last year of symptoms, was there any major stressful event or change in your life that seemed to trigger them?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. I mean, I've always been like this, like we talked about, but it's definitely been noticeably harder to manage for, I don't know, about a year and two months now? \n\nThat's right around the time this whole massive conflict with my in-laws started taking over, like, our entire family life. It's just been non-stop drama. My wife's sister, she decided to buy this fixer-upper house right down the street from us, which sounds great in theory, right? Family close by. But she expected me to basically be her free general contractor because I run a business. I told her, look, I do junk removal and logistics, I don't hang drywall. But she got incredibly offended, and then my mother-in-law got involved, and suddenly we're the bad guys because we won't spend our weekends putting up shiplap. \n\nShiplap is so overdone anyway, honestly. Every house you see on those home renovation shows has it now. My wife watches those channels constantly. We actually thought about flipping houses once, back before the market got totally crazy. We went to this seminar at a Holiday Inn out by the airport. You know, one of those things where they try to sell you a ten-thousand-dollar coaching package at the end? Total scam. But the coffee they had in the lobby was surprisingly good. Usually hotel coffee tastes like burnt dirt, but this was a really smooth dark roast. I actually asked the guy at the front desk what brand it was, but he had no idea. \n\nAnyway... wait, what was the question? Oh, right, the stress. Yeah, the in-law thing has just been a huge drain on everything for the last year or so, and it definitely made all this other stuff way harder to deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have your attention and restlessness been mainly getting worse because of the in-law conflict?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, the in-law stuff definitely made it harder to deal with, but it's not like it just started then. Looking back, this was around when I was a kid, too. My teachers were always moving my seat because I talked way too much, or I'd just get up without asking, or I'd blurt things out before they even finished the question. \n\nI remember my fifth-grade teacher, Mrs. Henderson, she used to put my desk literally right next to hers. She had this huge wooden desk, solid oak, I think. Actually, oak furniture is really making a massive comeback right now. We just bought this giant solid oak dining table for the house. We had to hire three guys just to carry it through the front door because it weighed like four hundred pounds. One of the guys actually scraped the doorframe, which was incredibly annoying because I had just paid a guy to paint the trim this specific shade of white. You wouldn't believe how many shades of white paint there are at the hardware store. My wife brought home like thirty little paper swatches—eggshell, alabaster, antique snow... it all looks exactly the same to me. I told her to just pick one so we could get it over with, but she spent three weeks deciding. \n\nAnyway, uh... what were we talking about? Oh, right, the in-laws and it getting worse. Yeah, so the stress with them just kind of amplified how I've always been since I was little.\n\nDoctor: Did these problems first begin after the conflict with your in-laws started?\n\nPatient: No, no, like I was just saying a second ago, it definitely didn't start with the in-laws. Looking back, this was totally around when I was a kid, too. My teachers were always moving my seat around the classroom because I talked way too much, or I'd just get up without asking, or I'd be blurting things out before they even finished asking the question. \n\nI spent half my time sitting out in the hallway because of it. Our school hallways always smelled like that really strong industrial floor wax, you know the kind? Like fake lemon and heavy chemicals. We actually use a similar industrial degreaser at the warehouse for my company. I run a hauling and logistics business, which is great, but keeping the warehouse floors clean is a total nightmare with the trucks pulling in and out all day. The tires track in so much mud, especially when it rains. It rained for like four days straight last week, completely ruined my weekend plans. I was supposed to take my boat out to the lake. It’s just a small aluminum fishing boat, but I just put a new outboard motor on it, a Yamaha. Those Yamaha motors are incredibly reliable, way better than the Mercury I had before. The old one kept stalling out right in the middle of the lake. \n\nWait, sorry. Did you just ask me the same question? But yeah, no, definitely not just the in-laws. Like I said, it's been happening since I was a little kid in school.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually energized or overly upbeat, with much less need for sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm pretty high-energy most of the time, but not like... unusually upbeat for days on end without sleeping. I definitely need my sleep. If I don't get at least six hours, I'm totally useless the next day. \n\nAlthough, honestly, getting decent sleep has been tough lately because we just got this new mattress. It's one of those memory foam ones that comes rolled up in a box, which is wild to me. How do they compress a whole king-size mattress into a box that small? The delivery guy practically threw his back out carrying it up our front steps. We had to wait 48 hours for it to fully expand before we could even sleep on it, which meant we were stuck on the air mattress in the guest room for two nights. Air mattresses are the absolute worst. They always deflate right in the middle, so you wake up folded in half like a taco. I actually tried to patch a hole in an air mattress once with duct tape. Turns out, duct tape does not fix everything, despite what my dad used to say. He used duct tape on the bumper of his old Ford pickup for like three years. It was this hideous silver stripe across the back of a red truck. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, no, I don't have periods where I'm just buzzing for days without sleep. Just my normal level of busy.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a lot of different things on most days?\n\nPatient: I mean, not really? I'm not a super anxious guy. I don't sit around worrying about a bunch of random things every day. I'm usually too busy doing stuff to just sit and worry. \n\nThough I guess I do worry a little bit about the supply chain issues with the business right now. It's been a total headache trying to get parts for our forklifts. We had one down for three weeks waiting on a hydraulic pump. Three weeks! And the guy at the parts distributor was zero help. He just kept saying it was stuck at the port. I actually drove down to the port once, years ago, just to see the shipping containers. It's insane how big those cranes are. They look like giant metal dinosaurs just picking up these massive boxes. My nephew is obsessed with dinosaurs right now. He's four, and he knows the names of all the weird ones, not just the T-Rex. He was telling me about one with a club on its tail... an Ankylosaurus, I think? I bought him this huge remote-control dinosaur for his birthday, but it was so loud it made his dog hide under the couch. The dog is a rescue, some kind of terrier mix, and he's terrified of loud noises. We had a terrier growing up, actually... \n\nWait, what was the question? Worrying? Yeah, no, I don't really worry about a ton of stuff on a daily basis. Just normal business stuff, you know?\n\nDoctor: On most days, do you feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge most days. I mean, I'm always moving, always doing something, but it's not an anxious feeling. It's just... I just have a lot of energy, you know? \n\nLike, I can't just sit still. If I sit still too long, my legs start bouncing. It drives my wife crazy when we watch TV. We were watching this documentary the other night about deep-sea diving, which is fascinating, by the way. The amount of pressure down there is unbelievable. They have to use these special submersibles with super thick glass. I don't think I could ever do that, being trapped in a tiny metal ball underwater. I get a little claustrophobic sometimes. Like when I have to crawl under the deck to check the pipes. We had a pipe freeze last winter and it cracked, water everywhere. I had to army-crawl through the mud with a flashlight in my teeth. Reminded me of when I used to play paintball with my buddies back in my twenties. We used to go out into the woods every weekend. I was actually pretty good at it, very quick on my feet. \n\nBut yeah, no, I'm not walking around feeling on edge or anything. Just, you know, always ready to go.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry a lot about many different things and have trouble controlling that worry?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said, I'm not a big worrier. I don't get stuck on things or feel like I can't control it. I mean, I have a lot on my plate, running the company and all, but I don't sit around chewing my fingernails over every little detail. If there's a problem, I just fix it and move on. \n\nLike last week, the landscaping guys we use at the main warehouse accidentally backed their riding mower into our chainlink fence. Smashed it pretty good. The owner of the landscaping company, Sal, he's a great guy though. He makes his own venison sausage. He actually brought me a whole cooler full of it last November. I've never really gotten into hunting myself, honestly. You have to sit perfectly still in a freezing cold tree stand for like, six hours straight just waiting for a deer to walk by. I would go absolutely out of my mind. I'd much rather be on a snowmobile or a dirt bike, you know? Something with a throttle. I actually rebuilt a 1998 Honda dirt bike in my garage last spring. Took it completely apart down to the frame, repainted it, the whole nine yards. Getting the carburetor tuned right was a nightmare, but man, it runs like a dream now. \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? About worrying? Yeah, no, I don't really have a problem with worrying too much. I just stay busy.\n\nDoctor: Did anything happen around the time these symptoms worsened that felt traumatic or overwhelming to you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, nothing traumatic or anything like that. Things have just always been kind of... fast-paced for me, I guess. I've always been this way, even back in high school. Well, the part of high school I actually went to before I dropped out to work full-time. \n\nI started out just sweeping floors at this auto shop, but I learned pretty quick. The owner, this guy named Mike, he had this incredible collection of vintage license plates covering the entire back wall of the garage. He said he'd been collecting them since he was a kid. He had one from every state, even Hawaii and Alaska. I always wondered how you get a car from Hawaii to the mainland. Like, do you put it on a boat? Or a really big plane? I guess a boat makes more sense. I've only been on a boat a few times, mostly just fishing on the lake. My buddy has this pontoon boat, and we took it out last 4th of July. We tried to barbecue on it, but the grill tipped over and all the hot dogs fell in the water. The fish probably loved it, though. \n\nBut yeah, no, nothing traumatic happened. It's just how I've always been. Always on the go.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
